The\n93\tpattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\n94\tparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n95\t\n96\t* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n97\t* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n98\t* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n99\t* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n100\t  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n101\t\n102\tThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n103\t:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\n104\texpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n105\t\"\"\"\n106\t\n107\t\n108\tclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n109\t    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n110\t\n111\t    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n112\t\n113\t    1. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        manifest_path: Path,\n        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n        )\n        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n\n\ndef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n\n    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not error.absolute_path:\n        return \"$\"\n    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n        if isinstance(segment, int):\n            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n        else:\n            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n\n    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n    except OSError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    try:\n        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n        violation = _Violation(\n            path=\"$\",\n            message=(\n                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n            ),\n        )\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n\n    return decoded\n\n\ndef _validate_manifest_dict(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read ``path``, decode YAML, and validate against ``suite.schema.json``.\n\n    Returns the raw manifest mapping (suite envelope + ``evals[]``) so\n    callers that need suite-level metadata (``name``, ``version``,\n    ``required_binaries``, etc.) — notably :class:`SuiteLoader` — do not\n    have to re-read or re-parse the file. This is the single ingress\n    point for FR-SCH1: every schema rejection raised inside the loader\n    flows through here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest = _read_manifest(path)\n\n    schema = _load_schema()\n    validator = Draft202012Validator(schema)\n    errors = sorted(\n        validator.iter_errors(manifest),\n        key=lambda e: (list(e.absolute_path), e.message),\n    )\n    if errors:\n        violations = [\n            _Violation(path=_format_json_path(err), message=err.message)\n            for err in errors\n        ]\n        raise SchemaError(path, violations)\n\n    return manifest\n\n\ndef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n        in the manifest. Order matches the manifest order so downstream\n        report ordering is deterministic.\n\n    Raises:\n        SchemaError: Raised when the manifest cannot be read, cannot be\n            decoded as YAML, decodes to a non-mapping, or violates any\n            constraint in ``suite.schema.json``. The exception carries\n            one violation entry per jsonschema error, each annotated\n            with a ``$.path.to.field`` JSON path. CLI callers MUST map\n            this to :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = Path(path)\n    manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n    return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry) for entry in manifest[\"evals\"]]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# COMP-002 SuiteLoader (T01.07 / D-0006)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nUNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`UnresolvedCapability` reaches the CLI.\n\nThe third member of the SuiteLoader error trio (alongside\n:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` and :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`). All\nthree map to ``2`` per the design-spec §4 exit-code table so operators see\na single \"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\"\noutcome regardless of which gate fired.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nSUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Single canonical exit code for every :class:`SuiteLoaderError` subclass.\n\n``SuiteLoader`` only ever exits with ``2`` because every loader-layer\nfailure is, from the operator's point of view, \"the harness refused to\nstart running the suite\". The per-class constants\n(:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`,\n:data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`) are kept so callers can branch on\nintent without coupling the failure classes; this constant is the\naggregate ``SuiteLoader``-boundary alias used by CLI tests.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnresolvedCapability(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a required capability cannot be satisfied at load time.\n\n    Surfaced by :class:`SuiteLoader` after :func:`validate_eval_id` has\n    accepted every static id but before parameterize expansion runs. We intentionally do not make\n# the three concrete classes inherit from a new base — the per-class\n# subclassing already keeps them disjoint, and a base class would force\n# downstream callers (and existing tests) to deal with a new ABC.\nSuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)\n\n\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Pluggable contract the SuiteLoader uses to gate ``requires`` clauses.\n\n    The Protocol intentionally has a single method so the M1 SuiteLoader\n    can be wired before COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates`` (T01.11) lands. The\n    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n    capability names (which the loader turns into\n    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n\n    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n\n        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n        \"\"\"\n        ...\n\n\nclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n\n    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n    no-op default. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n       network MCP-reachability probes.\n    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n\n    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n\n    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n        Args:\n            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n\n        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n22\t  stderr line names the offending manifest and the typed error class\n23\t  (`SchemaError`, `InvalidEvalId`, `UnresolvedCapability`) so operators\n24\t  can locate the failure without digging through stack traces.\n25\t\n26\t## Output schema\n27\t\n28\t### Human (default)\n29\t\n30\tRendered by `render_list_text(summaries)`:\n31\t\n32\t```\n33\tsuperclaude eval list:\n34\t  - <name> (version <version>, <count> eval[s])\n35\t  - <name> (version <version>, <count> eval[s])\n36\t```\n37\t\n38\tThe empty case prints `  (no suites found)`. Pluralisation toggles\n39\tbetween `eval` (1) and `evals` (0 or >=2) so the output reads\n40\tnaturally without losing parseability for downstream awk-style\n41\tconsumers.\n42\t\n43\t### JSON (`--json`)\n44\t\n45\t```json\n46\t[\n47\t  { \"name\": \"<string>\", \"version\": \"<string>\", \"eval_count\": <int> },\n48\t  …\n49\t]\n50\t```\n51\t\n52\t`json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` guarantees byte-level\n53\tdeterminism across invocations on a stable host (verified by\n54\t`test_cli_list_json_is_deterministic_across_invocations`).\n55\t\n56\t## Eval count semantics\n57\t\n58\t`eval_count` is the **post-parameterize-expansion** count\n59\t(`len(ParsedSuite.evals)`), not the raw `evals[]` length. This reflects\n60\tthe operator-facing question \"how many evals would run if I executed\n61\tthis suite?\" rather than the manifest's row count. A manifest with one\n62\tstatic row plus one parameterized row of three values reports\n63\t`eval_count: 4`, not `eval_count: 2`.\n64\t\n65\t## Discovery rules\n66\t\n67\t`discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir)` returns the glob\n68\t`suites_dir/*.yaml` sorted by filename:\n69\t\n70\t* Missing or non-directory `suites_dir` → empty list (no error). A\n71\t  fresh checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not\n72\t  break `eval list`.\n73\t* Non-YAML files (`README.md`, `suite.schema.json`) are filtered out by\n74\t  the `*.yaml` glob.\n75\t* The sort is alphabetical by filename, providing deterministic output\n76\t  regardless of filesystem iteration order.\n77\t\n78\t## Loader integration\n79\t\n80\t`summarize_suites(suites_dir, *, loader=None)` funnels every discovered\n81\tmanifest through `SuiteLoader.load()` so the same five-stage gate chain\n82\t(schema → static id regex → capability resolution → parameterize\n83\texpansion → expanded-id regex re-check) that protects `eval run`\n84\tgates `eval list` too. There is no separate fast-path that bypasses\n85\tFR-SCH2 — listing an unsafe manifest fails closed with exit 2.\n86\t\n87\tThe `loader` parameter is injectable for tests that want to stub schema\n88\tor capability gates without touching the file system.\n89\t\n90\t## Test injection seams\n91\t\n92\t| Seam | How tests use it |\n93\t|---|---|\n94\t| `--suites-dir` | Fixture suites are copied into a tmp dir per test; the override avoids polluting the package suites directory. |\n95\t| `summarize_suites(loader=...)` | Inject a stubbed `SuiteLoader` for tests that need full control over schema / regex / capability gates. |\n96\t| `discover_suite_manifests` | Pure function over `Path`; tests call it directly on tmp dirs to verify the sort + filter contracts. |\n97\t\n98\t## Wiring\n99\t\n100\t* `eval_list` is registered on the existing `eval_group` (`@eval_group.command(\"list\")`).\n101\t* `eval_group` is already wired into `superclaude.cli.main` via\n102\t  `main.add_command(eval_group, name=\"eval\")` from T01.13 — no new\n103\t  top-level wiring required.\n104\t\n105\t## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n106\t\n107\t| AC bullet (T01.21) | Implementation site |\n108\t|---|---|\n109\t| `superclaude eval list` exits 0 with at least one suite present and zero suites present (empty-directory case). | `eval_list` Click handler + `render_list_text` empty branch; Resolution precedence below. |\n| `--eval` | string | no | Filter output to one post-expansion eval id. Missing id → `EvalNotFound` (exit 2). |\n| `--json` | bool flag | no | Emit JSON. Default output is YAML (block style, unicode-friendly). |\n| `--suites-dir` | path | no | Override the directory used for name / stem lookups. Defaults to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/`. |\n\n### Exit codes\n\n| Code | Trigger |\n|---|---|\n| `0` | Manifest validated and printed. |\n| `2` (`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) | Schema / id-regex / capability rejection. Stderr names the typed error class (`SchemaError`, `InvalidEvalId`, `UnresolvedCapability`). |\n| `2` (`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`) | `--suite` token did not resolve to any manifest. Stderr names `SuiteNotFound` and the searched directory. |\n| `2` (`EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`) | `--eval` id did not match any post-expansion id. Stderr names `EvalNotFound`, the suite name, and the known id list. |\n\nAll three \"rejection\" codes are intentionally `2` so operators see a\nsingle \"harness rejected the describe request\" outcome regardless of\nwhich gate fired. Per-class constants are kept so call sites and tests\ncan branch on intent.\n\n## `--suite` resolution\n\n`resolve_suite_manifest(suite, suites_dir)` walks three rules in order\n(first match wins):\n\n1. `suite` is itself an existing file path → return it verbatim. Lets\n   operators describe an out-of-tree manifest by path without copying\n   it into `suites_dir`.\n2. `suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"` exists → return it. Matches the\n   filename-stem convention used by built-in suites that will land in\n   later milestones.\n3. Scan `discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir)` and return the first\n   manifest whose schema-validated `name:` field equals `suite`.\n   Broken neighbours are skipped silently — only the matching manifest\n   needs to validate.\n\nIf no rule matches, `SuiteNotFound` is raised and the CLI exits 2 with\nthe typed class name on stderr.\n\n## Output schema\n\n### Default (YAML)\n\n`render_describe_yaml(payload)` renders block-style YAML with\n`sort_keys=False` so the field ordering matches `suite.schema.json`\n(envelope: `name`, `version`, `description`, `defaults`,\n`required_binaries`, `optional_capabilities`, `evals`). `allow_unicode=True`\npreserves em-dashes and other non-ASCII characters that appear in real\nmanifest titles.\n\n### `--json`\n\n`render_describe_json(payload)` emits `json.dumps(payload, indent=2,\nsort_keys=True)` so the JSON output is byte-stable across hosts and\nPython versions (verified by `test_render_describe_json_is_deterministic`\nand `test_cli_describe_yaml_is_deterministic`).\n\n### Envelope shape (no `--eval`)\n\n`_parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)` projects a `ParsedSuite` into:\n\n```yaml\nname: <string>\nversion: <string>\ndescription: <string>\ndefaults: <mapping>\nrequired_binaries: [ <requiredBinary>, ... ]\noptional_capabilities: [ <optionalCapability>, ... ]\nevals: [ <evalEntry>, ... ]   # post-parameterize-expansion\n```\n\n`evals` is the **post-parameterize-expansion** list (`parsed.evals`),\nnot the raw `evals[]` length. A manifest with one static row plus one\nparameterized row of three values prints four `evals[]` entries with\nids `E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3`.\n\n### Single-eval projection (`--eval <id>`)\n\n`_evalspec_to_dict(spec)` projects an `EvalSpec` into the\n`evalEntry` shape (no suite envelope):\n\n```yaml\nid: <string>             # already FR-SCH2-validated post-expansion\ntitle: <string>\ncategory: <string>       # omitted when \"\"\nrequires: [<string>, ...]   # omitted when empty\ntimeout_sec: <int>       # omitted when None\nisolation: <mapping>     # omitted when None\ninputs: [<mapping>, ...] # omitted when empty\nexpects: [<mapping>, ...] # omitted when empty\nparameterize: [<mapping>, ...]  # preserved on expanded entries\n```\n\nOptional fields are omitted when they hold the dataclass default\n(`category=\"\"`, empty sequences, `timeout_sec=None`). This honours the\nT01.22 Notes contract: \"Output mirrors validated manifest exactly; no\neditorial transformation\" — an eval that did not declare `category:`\nin YAML does not gain one in the description.\n\n## Validation order (FR-CLI3 AC)\n\n`describe_suite()` runs the full `SuiteLoader.load()` gate chain\n**before any rendering**:\n\n1. Resolve `--suite` to a manifest path (no FS write).\n2. `SuiteLoader.load()` — schema (FR-SCH1) → static-id regex\n   (FR-SCH2) → capability resolution (COMP-009 surface) →\n   parameterize expansion → expanded-id regex re-check (FR-SCH2).\n3. Optional `--eval` id lookup against the post-expansion list.\n4. Render YAML / JSON.\n\nValidation rejections raise from step 2 — `click.echo` of the payload\nin step 4 is never reached. Verified by\n`test_cli_describe_validation_runs_before_any_stdout`, which asserts\n`result.stdout == \"\"` on a schema-rejection path.\n\n## Loader integration\n\n`describe_suite(suite, *, suites_dir, eval_id=None, loader=None)` is\nthe function the CLI handler delegates to. The `loader` parameter is\ninjectable so tests can stub `SuiteLoader` without touching the file\nsystem. The default loader uses `PermissiveCapabilityResolver` (same\nas `eval list`) — capability gating for `describe` is intentionally\npermissive because describing a manifest is a read-only operation that\nshould not require the runtime binary set on the host.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.22) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `superclaude eval describe --suite <name>` prints validated post-parameterize manifest content. | `eval_describe` Click handler + `describe_suite` + `render_describe_yaml`; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01UkHEuuFYYSd3qK15c3Jj3n","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_011bC9V4Uxc5MWdkWobuHroR","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0019/notes.md","content":"# D-0019 — implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n### Three-rule `--suite` resolution\n\nThe acceptance criterion `describe --suite <name>` is loose about what\n\"name\" means. We support three resolution rules so operators can use\nthe most natural reference:\n\n1. **Direct path** — `superclaude eval describe --suite ./my-suite.yaml`.\n   Useful when developing a manifest outside `cli/eval/suites/`.\n2. **Filename stem** — `--suite reference` resolves to\n   `<suites-dir>/reference.yaml`. Cheap (no manifest load) and matches\n   the convention built-in suites will follow.\n3. **`name:` field** — last resort: scan and match the manifest's\n   declared `name`. Required for fixtures like `valid_suite.yaml` whose\n   filename diverges from the manifest name (`reference`).\n\nRule 3 silently skips broken neighbours so a single bad manifest in\n`suites_dir` does not block lookups of unrelated suites.\n\n### Two-shape output\n\n`describe` returns one of two shapes depending on `--eval`:\n\n- **Envelope** (no `--eval`) — the full manifest projection. Matches\n  the schema's top-level shape (`name`, `version`, `description`,\n  `defaults`, `required_binaries`, `optional_capabilities`, `evals`).\n- **Single entry** (with `--eval`) — just the `evalEntry` projection.\n  No suite envelope. This is the operator-facing answer to \"what would\n  run if I executed eval X?\" and matches the `manifest.evals[i]` shape\n  one-to-one.\n\nThe renderer functions (`render_describe_yaml`, `render_describe_json`)\ndon't care which shape they receive — they emit any dict.\n\n### Omitting default optional fields\n\n`_evalspec_to_dict` omits fields that hold their dataclass default:\n\n- `category=\"\"` → omitted\n- `requires=()`, `inputs=()`, `expects=()`, `parameterize=()` → omitted\n- `timeout_sec=None`, `isolation=None` → omitted\n\nRationale: T01.22 Notes says \"Output mirrors validated manifest\nexactly; no editorial transformation.\" An eval that did not declare\n`category:` in YAML should not gain a `category: \"\"` line in the\ndescription output. Required fields (`id`, `title`) always appear.\n\n### Validation BEFORE rendering\n\nThe Click handler runs the full `SuiteLoader.load()` gate chain inside\n`describe_suite`, so any schema / id-regex / capability rejection\nraises an exception that the `try/except` block maps to exit 2 BEFORE\n`click.echo` of the payload is reached. Verified by snapshot test\n`test_cli_describe_validation_runs_before_any_stdout`.\n\n### YAML output settings\n\n`yaml.safe_dump(payload, sort_keys=False, default_flow_style=False,\nallow_unicode=True)`:\n\n- `sort_keys=False` preserves the field order our projection produces\n  (matches `suite.schema.json` declaration order).\n- `default_flow_style=False` keeps block style throughout so the YAML\n  reads like the source manifest (no inline `{a: 1, b: 2}` collapses).\n- `allow_unicode=True` keeps em-dashes and other non-ASCII characters\n  in titles intact instead of escaping them to `—` etc.\n\n### JSON output determinism\n\n`json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` matches the\n`eval list --json` contract. Byte-stable across hosts and Python\nversions; verified by the determinism test.\n\n## Test seams\n\nTests inject a `tmp_path` via `--suites-dir`, copy fixture YAML files\ninto it, and exercise both the function-level API (`describe_suite`,\n`resolve_suite_manifest`, `_evalspec_to_dict`, `_parsed_suite_to_dict`)\nand the Click CLI surface via `CliRunner`.\n\n## Cross-cuts\n\n- **FR-SCH1 (T01.04 / R-004)** — schema validation runs via\n  `SuiteLoader.load()`, which delegates to `validate_manifest`. Schema\n  rejection → exit 2 with `SchemaError` on stderr.\n- **FR-SCH2 (T01.05 / R-005)** — eval-id regex runs pre- AND\n  post-expansion inside `SuiteLoader`. We support three resolution rules so operators can use\nthe most natural reference:\n\n1. **Direct path** — `superclaude eval describe --suite ./my-suite.yaml`.\n   Useful when developing a manifest outside `cli/eval/suites/`.\n2. **Filename stem** — `--suite reference` resolves to\n   `<suites-dir>/reference.yaml`. Cheap (no manifest load) and matches\n   the convention built-in suites will follow.\n3. **`name:` field** — last resort: scan and match the manifest's\n   declared `name`. Required for fixtures like `valid_suite.yaml` whose\n   filename diverges from the manifest name (`reference`).\n\nRule 3 silently skips broken neighbours so a single bad manifest in\n`suites_dir` does not block lookups of unrelated suites.\n\n### Two-shape output\n\n`describe` returns one of two shapes depending on `--eval`:\n\n- **Envelope** (no `--eval`) — the full manifest projection. Matches\n  the schema's top-level shape (`name`, `version`, `description`,\n  `defaults`, `required_binaries`, `optional_capabilities`, `evals`).\n- **Single entry** (with `--eval`) — just the `evalEntry` projection.\n  No suite envelope. This is the operator-facing answer to \"what would\n  run if I executed eval X?\" and matches the `manifest.evals[i]` shape\n  one-to-one.\n\nThe renderer functions (`render_describe_yaml`, `render_describe_json`)\ndon't care which shape they receive — they emit any dict.\n\n### Omitting default optional fields\n\n`_evalspec_to_dict` omits fields that hold their dataclass default:\n\n- `category=\"\"` → omitted\n- `requires=()`, `inputs=()`, `expects=()`, `parameterize=()` → omitted\n- `timeout_sec=None`, `isolation=None` → omitted\n\nRationale: T01.22 Notes says \"Output mirrors validated manifest\nexactly; no editorial transformation.\" An eval that did not declare\n`category:` in YAML should not gain a `category: \"\"` line in the\ndescription output. Required fields (`id`, `title`) always appear.\n\n### Validation BEFORE rendering\n\nThe Click handler runs the full `SuiteLoader.load()` gate chain inside\n`describe_suite`, so any schema / id-regex / capability rejection\nraises an exception that the `try/except` block maps to exit 2 BEFORE\n`click.echo` of the payload is reached. Verified by snapshot test\n`test_cli_describe_validation_runs_before_any_stdout`.\n\n### YAML output settings\n\n`yaml.safe_dump(payload, sort_keys=False, default_flow_style=False,\nallow_unicode=True)`:\n\n- `sort_keys=False` preserves the field order our projection produces\n  (matches `suite.schema.json` declaration order).\n- `default_flow_style=False` keeps block style throughout so the YAML\n  reads like the source manifest (no inline `{a: 1, b: 2}` collapses).\n- `allow_unicode=True` keeps em-dashes and other non-ASCII characters\n  in titles intact instead of escaping them to `—` etc.\n\n### JSON output determinism\n\n`json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` matches the\n`eval list --json` contract. Byte-stable across hosts and Python\nversions; verified by the determinism test.\n\n## Test seams\n\nTests inject a `tmp_path` via `--suites-dir`, copy fixture YAML files\ninto it, and exercise both the function-level API (`describe_suite`,\n`resolve_suite_manifest`, `_evalspec_to_dict`, `_parsed_suite_to_dict`)\nand the Click CLI surface via `CliRunner`.\n\n## Cross-cuts\n\n- **FR-SCH1 (T01.04 / R-004)** — schema validation runs via\n  `SuiteLoader.load()`, which delegates to `validate_manifest`. Schema\n  rejection → exit 2 with `SchemaError` on stderr.\n- **FR-SCH2 (T01.05 / R-005)** — eval-id regex runs pre- AND\n  post-expansion inside `SuiteLoader`.
